<tr><td>↳近 groom</td><td>/ˈɡruːm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>打扮修饰；梳理</td><td>groom oneself carefully<br>仔细打扮自己</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He groomed himself carefully before the important interview.  重要面试前他仔细打扮了自己。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plume</td><td>/ˈpluːm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>得意地打扮；羽毛</td><td>plume oneself on success<br>因成功而自我炫耀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bird plumed its feathers on the sunny branch.  那只鸟在阳光下的树枝上梳理羽毛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 primp</td><td>/prɪmp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>精心打扮</td><td>primp for the party<br>为聚会精心打扮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spent an hour primping in front of the mirror before the party.  聚会前她在镜子前精心打扮了一个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>preface</b></td><td>/ˈprefəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>序言</td><td>write a preface<br>写序言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The author used the preface to thank everyone who helped her research.  作者在序言中感谢了所有帮助她做研究的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exordium</td><td>/ɛɡˈzɔrdiəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>开场白，绪言</td><td>a brief exordium<br>简短的开场白</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speaker began with a brief exordium praising his hosts.  演讲者以一段简短开场白称赞主办方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foreword</td><td>/ˈfɔːrwɜːrd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>前言</td><td>write the foreword<br>撰写前言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A famous historian wrote the foreword to her first book.  一位著名历史学家为她的第一本书撰写了前言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 introduction</td><td>/ˌɪntrəˈdʌkʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>引言，导论；介绍</td><td>a brief introduction<br>简短的引言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book opens with a brief introduction by the editor.  这本书以编者的简短引言开篇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overture</td><td>/ˈoʊvərtʃər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>序曲，前奏；提议</td><td>play the overture<br>演奏序曲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The orchestra opened the evening with a lively overture by Rossini.  乐团以罗西尼一首轻快的序曲开始了晚会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preamble</td><td>/ˈpriːæmbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>序言，开场白</td><td>the preamble to the treaty<br>条约的序言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The preamble to the treaty states its guiding principles.  条约的序言阐明了其指导原则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prelude</td><td>/ˈpreɪˌluːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>序曲；前奏，先兆</td><td>a prelude to war<br>战争的前奏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The border clashes proved to be a prelude to full-scale war.  边境冲突后来成了全面战争的前奏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prologue</td><td>/ˈproʊlɔːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>序言</td><td>the prologue to the play<br>该剧的序言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prologue to the play explains how the bitter feud began.  该剧的序言说明了这场激烈仇怨的由来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 epilogue</td><td>/ˈepɪlɔːɡ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>文学作品的结局</td><td>a brief epilogue<br>简短的结语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brief epilogue tells the reader what became of the two brothers.  简短的结语告诉读者两兄弟后来的境况。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pregnant</b></td><td>/ˈpreɡnənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，意味深长的</td><td>a pregnant pause<br>意味深长的停顿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her answer was followed by a pregnant pause that unsettled the room.  她的回答之后是一段意味深长的停顿，令全场不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eloquent</td><td>/ˈeləkwənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>雄辩的，有说服力的</td><td>an eloquent speech<br>雄辩的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His eloquent defense of the plan won several extra votes.  他对该计划雄辩的辩护多赢得了几张选票。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meaningful</td><td>/ˈmiːnɪŋfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有意义的，意味深长的</td><td>meaningful work<br>有意义的工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left banking to find more meaningful work.  她离开银行业去寻找更有意义的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 momentous</td><td>/moʊˈmentəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重大的，重要的</td><td>a momentous decision<br>一个重大的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board reached a momentous decision that changed the company's direction entirely.  董事会做出了一个重大决定，彻底改变了公司的方向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profound</td><td>/proʊˈfaʊnd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深刻的，深远的</td><td>a profound influence<br>深远的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her death had a profound effect on the whole family.  她的死对整个家庭产生了深远的影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 revelatory</td><td>/rɪˈveləˌtɔːri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>揭示性的，启发性的</td><td>a revelatory detail<br>极具揭示性的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One revelatory detail in the letter changed the whole story.  信中一个揭示性的细节改变了整个故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 significant</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；显著的</td><td>a significant difference<br>显著的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a significant drop in the pollution levels.  研究者发现污染水平显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suggestive</td><td>/səˈdʒestɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>引人联想的，暗示的</td><td>a suggestive silence<br>意味深长的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His suggestive silence told the reporters more than words.  他意味深长的沉默比言语告诉记者的更多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inane</td><td>/ɪˈneɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>空洞的</td><td>inane chatter<br>空洞的闲聊</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's dialogue was inane and endlessly repetitive.  这部电影的对白空洞且不断重复。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>premeditate</b></del></td><td>/prɪˈmedɪteɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先考虑</td><td>premeditate the crime<br>预谋犯罪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer argued that his client did not premeditate the attack.  律师主张他的委托人并未预先谋划这场袭击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deliberate</td><td>/dɪˈlɪbərət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>深思熟虑的，故意的</td><td>a deliberate attempt<br>故意的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delay was deliberate, designed to weaken our position.  这次延误是故意的，目的是削弱我们的立场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 prearrange</del></td><td>/ˌpriːəˈreɪndʒ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先安排</td><td>prearrange a meeting<br>预先安排一次会面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They prearranged a meeting at the station for six o'clock.  他们预先安排六点在车站会面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prepare</td><td>/priˈper/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>准备，预备</td><td>prepare in advance<br>提前做好准备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team prepared for the debate weeks in advance.  这个队伍提前数周为辩论做准备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 preplan</del></td><td>/ˌpriˈplæn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预先计划</td><td>preplan every detail<br>预先规划每个细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She preplanned every detail of the long research trip.  她预先规划了这次长途考察的每个细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disregard</td><td>/ˌdɪsrɪˈɡɑːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>漠视，不关注；缺乏兴趣，缺乏关心</td><td>total disregard for safety<br>完全不顾安全</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their total disregard for safety rules eventually caused a serious accident.  他们完全不顾安全规定，最终造成了一起严重事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ignore</td><td>/ˌɪɡˈnɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视，不理</td><td>ignore the warning<br>忽视警告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The captain ignored the warning and sailed into the storm.  船长忽视了警告，驶入了风暴之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 neglect</td><td>/nəˈɡlekt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>忽视，疏忽；忽略，怠慢</td><td>neglect his duties<br>疏忽职责</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He neglected his duties for weeks and was finally dismissed.  他数周疏忽职责，最终被解雇了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 omit</td><td>/oʊˈmɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>省略，遗漏，忽略</td><td>omit the details<br>省略细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The summary omits the details that made his argument convincing.  摘要省略了使他论证有说服力的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 overlook</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌlʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>忽视；宽恕；俯视</td><td>overlook a minor error<br>宽容小错</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher chose to overlook the boy's late arrival.  老师决定不追究那男孩的迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>preoccupation</b></td><td>/priˌɑːkjuˈpeɪʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>全神贯注；念念不忘之事</td><td>a preoccupation with money<br>一心只想着钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her preoccupation with the exam kept her awake all night.  她一心想着考试，整夜没睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 absorption</td><td>/əbˈzɔːrpʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>专注，全神贯注；吸收</td><td>complete absorption in work<br>全然投入工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His absorption in the puzzle made him forget dinner entirely.  他专注于那道谜题，把晚饭忘得一干二净。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 engagement</td><td>/enˈɡeɪdʒmənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>投入，专注；约定</td><td>deep engagement with the text<br>对文本的深度投入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her engagement with the material impressed the whole seminar.  她对材料的投入让整个研讨班印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 engrossment</del></td><td>/ɪnˈɡroʊsmənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>全神贯注，入迷</td><td>total engrossment in a book<br>完全沉浸于一本书</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His engrossment in the novel lasted until well past midnight.  他沉迷这本小说直到深夜过后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immersion</td><td>/ɪˈmɜːrʒən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>沉浸，专心投入</td><td>complete immersion in study<br>完全沉浸于学习</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A month of total immersion improved her Japanese dramatically.  一个月的完全沉浸让她的日语大幅提高。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 apathy</td><td>/ˈæpəθi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>缺乏兴趣，不关心</td><td>public apathy toward voting<br>公众对投票的冷漠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Widespread apathy among young voters worried the campaign organizers.  年轻选民中普遍的冷漠让竞选组织者担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 indifference</td><td>/ɪnˈdɪfərəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>漠不关心，冷淡</td><td>treat with indifference<br>以冷淡的态度对待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd greeted the announcement with total indifference.  人群对这个通告完全漠不关心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nonchalance</td><td>/ˌnɑːnʃəˈlɑːns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冷漠，漠不关心</td><td>studied nonchalance<br>刻意的淡然</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She accepted the important award with surprising nonchalance.  她以出人意料的淡然接受了这个重要奖项。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unconcern</del></td><td>/ˌʌnkənˈsɜːrn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不关心，漠不关心</td><td>apparent unconcern for others<br>对他人明显的漠视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unconcern about the deadline annoyed the whole team.  他对截止日期的漠不关心让整个团队恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>preponderant</b></td><td>/prɪˈpɑndərənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>占优势的，主要的</td><td>the preponderant influence<br>占主导的影响力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trade remained the preponderant concern in every round of talks.  贸易在每一轮会谈中都是最主要的议题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dominant</td><td>/ˈdɑːmɪnənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>处于支配地位的；（基因）显性的</td><td>the dominant player<br>占主导地位的一方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dominant player in the market set prices that everyone else followed.  市场上占主导地位的那家定的价，其他人都跟着走。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 paramount</td><td>/ˈpærəmaʊnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>最重要的</td><td>of paramount importance<br>至为重要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Safety is paramount when workers handle these highly flammable chemicals.  工人处理这些极易燃的化学品时，安全最为重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 predominant</td><td>/prɪˈdɑːmənənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>主要的，占优势的</td><td>the predominant view<br>占主导的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rice remains the predominant crop in the southern valleys.  稻米仍是南部谷地的主要作物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prevalent</td><td>/ˈprevələnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>流行的，普遍的</td><td>a prevalent belief<br>普遍的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The belief that talent is innate remains surprisingly prevalent among teachers.  认为天赋是天生的这种看法在教师中仍出奇地普遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overruling</td><td>/ˈoʊvərˌruːlɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>压倒一切的；驳回</td><td>an overruling concern<br>压倒一切的关切</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Safety was the overruling concern in every design decision.  安全是每项设计决定中压倒一切的关切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 secondary</td><td>/ˈsekənˌderi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>次要的，从属的</td><td>of secondary importance<br>重要性次之</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cost was secondary to safety in the final decision.  在最终决定中，成本次于安全。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 subsidiary</td><td>/səbˈsɪdieri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>附属的，次要的</td><td>a subsidiary issue<br>次要的问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company sold its subsidiary business to focus on core products.  公司出售了其附属业务，以专注于核心产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prepossessing</b></td><td>/ˌpriːpəˈzesɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>给人好感的，有魅力的</td><td>a prepossessing manner<br>讨人喜欢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young lawyer had a prepossessing smile that quickly won over the jury.  这位年轻律师有着讨人喜欢的笑容，很快赢得了陪审团的好感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alluring</td><td>/əˈlʊrɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>诱人的，有魅力的</td><td>an alluring smile<br>迷人的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The alluring aroma of fresh bread drew us into the bakery.  新鲜面包诱人的香气把我们引进了面包店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attractive</td><td>/əˈtræktɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，迷人的</td><td>an attractive offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made him an attractive offer he could not refuse.  公司给了他一个无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appealing</td><td>/əˈpiːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的</td><td>an appealing offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made an appealing offer that she could hardly refuse.  公司提出了一个她几乎无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 captivating</td><td>/ˈkæptɪˌveɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，令人着迷的</td><td>a captivating performance<br>令人着迷的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her captivating voice held the entire audience in complete silence.  她迷人的歌声让全场观众鸦雀无声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charming</td><td>/ˈtʃɑːrmɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，可爱的</td><td>a charming little village<br>迷人的小村庄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent the afternoon in a charming village by the lake.  我们在湖边一个迷人的村庄度过了下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enchanting</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃæntɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，醉人的</td><td>an enchanting melody<br>醉人的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden looked enchanting under the soft light of lanterns.  花园在灯笼柔和的光线下显得十分迷人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pleasing</td><td>/ˈpliːzɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悦耳的，令人愉快的</td><td>a pleasing melody<br>悦耳的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her voice carried a pleasing warmth that calmed the children.  她的声音带着悦耳的温暖，让孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 riveting</td><td>/ˈrɪvɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的，极迷人的</td><td>a riveting account<br>引人入胜的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The documentary offers a riveting account of life inside the mountain villages.  这部纪录片对山村生活的叙述引人入胜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 abhorrent</td><td>/æbˈhɔːrənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>abhorrent to most people<br>令多数人厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The practice is abhorrent to anyone with basic decency.  这种做法令任何有基本良知的人厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appalling</td><td>/əˈpɔːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>骇人的，糟糕透顶的</td><td>appalling living conditions<br>骇人的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers lived in appalling conditions close to the mine.  工人们住在矿区附近骇人的条件中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disgusting</td><td>/dɪsˈɡʌstɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a disgusting smell<br>令人恶心的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was left in a disgusting state after the party.  聚会后厨房脏得让人恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 hideous</td><td>/ˈhɪdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常丑陋的</td><td>a hideous mask<br>丑陋可怕的面具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tore down the hideous concrete tower that blocked the view.  他们拆掉了那座挡住视野的丑陋混凝土塔楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 loathsome</td><td>/ˈloʊðsəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>a loathsome smell<br>令人厌恶的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A loathsome smell rose from the neglected drain.  无人清理的下水道升起令人厌恶的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repellent</td><td>/rɪˈpelənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>令人厌恶的；驱虫剂</td><td>a repellent odor<br>令人厌恶的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found his arrogant remarks utterly repellent and left early.  我觉得他傲慢的言论令人极其厌恶，于是提前离开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repulsive</td><td>/rɪˈpʌlsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a repulsive sight<br>令人恶心的景象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rotting fish on the beach was absolutely repulsive.  沙滩上腐烂的鱼令人极其恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>preposterous</b></td><td>/prɪˈpɑːstərəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>荒谬的，极其反常的</td><td>a preposterous claim<br>荒唐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The idea that he acted alone is preposterous.  说他独自行动的说法荒谬至极。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 absurd</td><td>/əbˈsɜːrd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合理的，荒谬的</td><td>an absurd suggestion<br>荒谬的建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It's absurd to expect results in a single week.  期望一周内出结果是荒谬的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 asinine</td><td>/ˈæsəˌnaɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，荒唐的</td><td>an asinine remark<br>愚蠢的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal was dismissed as an asinine waste of money.  该提案被斥为对金钱的愚蠢浪费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fallacious</td><td>/fəˈleɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谬误的，不合逻辑的</td><td>a fallacious argument<br>谬误的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer exposed the fallacious reasoning behind the entire claim.  律师揭穿了整个主张背后的谬误推理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fatuous</td><td>/ˈfætʃuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚笨的，昏庸的</td><td>a fatuous remark<br>愚蠢的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fatuous comment showed he had not read the report.  他愚蠢的评论说明他没读过那份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lunatic</td><td>/ˈluːnəˌtɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>疯狂的；疯子</td><td>a lunatic plan<br>疯狂的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Driving at that speed in fog is simply lunatic.  在雾中以那种速度开车简直疯狂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ludicrous</td><td>/ˈluːdɪkrəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>荒唐的，可笑的</td><td>a ludicrous idea<br>可笑的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The price they asked was simply ludicrous.  他们的要价简直可笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insane</td><td>/ɪnˈseɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>荒唐的，疯狂的</td><td>an insane plan<br>疯狂的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Driving in this thick fog would be insane.  在这种大雾里开车太疯狂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irrational</td><td>/ˌɪˈræʃənəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不理性的，无理性的</td><td>an irrational fear<br>非理性的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has an irrational fear of elevators and always takes stairs.  他对电梯有非理性的恐惧，总是走楼梯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unreasonable</td><td>/ənˈriːznəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合理的，过分的</td><td>unreasonable demands<br>不合理的要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Asking staff to work three weekends running is unreasonable.  要求员工连续三个周末加班是不合理的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 commonsensical</del></td><td>/ˌkɑːmənˈsensɪkl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>合乎常理的，明智的</td><td>a commonsensical approach<br>合乎常理的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His advice was commonsensical: sleep more and worry less.  他的建议合乎常理：多睡觉，少操心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>presage</b></td><td>/ˈpresɪdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>征兆</td><td>presage of disaster<br>灾难的征兆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden silence of the birds was a presage of the storm.  鸟鸣骤然停止，是暴风雨来临的征兆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 augury</td><td>/ˈɔɡjəri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>预兆；占卜</td><td>a good augury for the future<br>未来的好预兆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clear sunrise was taken as a favorable augury.  晴朗的日出被视为吉兆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 auspice</del></td><td>/ˈɔːspɪs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>预兆，吉兆；主办</td><td>under favorable auspices<br>在吉利的兆头下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The voyage began under remarkably favorable auspices that spring.  那年春天航行在极为吉利的兆头下启程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boding</td><td>/ˈboʊdɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>预兆，预感</td><td>an ill boding<br>不祥的预兆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The still air carried an ill boding of the coming storm.  凝滞的空气带着风暴将至的不祥预兆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foreboding</td><td>/fɔːrˈboʊdɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不祥的；不祥的预感</td><td>a sense of foreboding<br>不祥的预感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt a strange foreboding as the sky darkened.  天色转暗时她有种奇怪的不祥预感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foreshadowing</td><td>/fɔrˈʃædoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>预示，铺垫伏笔</td><td>subtle foreshadowing<br>细微的伏笔预示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The opening chapter is full of subtle foreshadowing of disaster.  开篇一章充满了对灾难的细微伏笔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 portent</td><td>/ˈpɔːrtent/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>预兆，凶兆</td><td>a dark portent<br>阴暗的凶兆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers read the comet as a dark portent of war.  村民们把这颗彗星看作战争的阴暗凶兆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prefiguring</td><td>/ˌpriˈfɪɡjərɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>预示，预先显现</td><td>a prefiguring of later work<br>对后期作品的预示</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These sketches are a clear prefiguring of his later murals.  这些草图明显预示了他后来的壁画。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 adumbrate</del></td><td>/ˈædəmbreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预示</td><td>adumbrate future trouble<br>预示日后的麻烦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The early chapters adumbrate the tragedy that follows.  开头几章预示了随后的悲剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 augur</td><td>/ˈɔːɡər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>预言家，占卜官；预言</td><td>augur well for the future<br>预示前景良好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The Roman augur studied the flight of birds before every battle.  这位罗马占卜官在每场战役前都观察鸟的飞行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forecast</td><td>/ˈfɔːrˌkæst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>预测，预报</td><td>forecast heavy rain<br>预报大雨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Economists forecast slower growth for the coming two years.  经济学家预测未来两年增长会放缓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foretell</td><td>/fɔrˈtel/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>预言，预示</td><td>foretell the outcome<br>预言结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No economist foretold the collapse of that entire market.  没有经济学家预言到整个市场的崩溃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 portend</td><td>/pɔːrˈtend/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预示，预兆</td><td>portend disaster<br>预示灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Falling bond prices may portend a wider financial crisis.  债券价格下跌可能预示更广泛的金融危机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 predict</td><td>/prɪˈdɪkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预言，预测</td><td>predict the outcome<br>预测结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one could predict how the jury would finally vote.  没人能预测陪审团最终会如何表决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 prognosticate</del></td><td>/ˌprɑːɡˈnɑːstəkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>预测，预言</td><td>prognosticate future trends<br>预测未来趋势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Analysts hesitate to prognosticate about markets in such uncertain times.  在如此不确定的时期分析师不愿预测市场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prophesy</td><td>/ˈprɑfəsaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>预言，预示</td><td>prophesy the fall of empires<br>预言帝国的衰亡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old monk prophesied that the city would burn twice.  老僧预言这座城市将两度被焚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prescience</b></td><td>/ˈpresiəns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>预知，先见</td><td>remarkable prescience<br>非凡的先见之明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His essay showed remarkable prescience about the coming crisis.  他的文章对即将到来的危机展现出非凡的先见之明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foresight</td><td>/ˈfɔːrˌsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>远见，预见力</td><td>show great foresight<br>表现出很强的远见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor showed great foresight in preserving the old harbor.  市长在保护老港口上表现出很强的远见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forethought</td><td>/ˈfɔːrˌθɔːt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>事先考虑，深谋远虑</td><td>careful forethought<br>周密的事先考虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With a little forethought the accident could have been avoided.  只要稍加事先考虑，这场事故本可避免。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 providence</td><td>/ˈprɑːvədəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>远见，深谋；天意</td><td>act with providence<br>有远见地行事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their providence in storing grain saved the village that winter.  他们储粮的远见在那个冬天救了村子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 improvidence</del></td><td>/ɪmˈprɑvədəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无远见，不节俭</td><td>financial improvidence<br>理财上的短视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of improvidence left the family with nothing saved.  多年的挥霍无谋让这家人毫无积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 myopia</td><td>/maɪˈoʊpiə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>短视，缺乏远见；近视</td><td>political myopia<br>政治上的短视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics blamed the crisis on the political myopia of leaders.  批评者把危机归咎于领导人的政治短视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 shortsightedness</td><td>/ˈʃɔːrtsaɪtɪdnɪs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>短视，缺乏远见</td><td>sheer shortsightedness<br>纯粹的短视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cutting research funding was pure shortsightedness on their part.  削减研究经费纯属他们的短视之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 5

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prescription</b></td><td>/prɪˈskrɪpʃn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>规定，指令；处方</td><td>the prescription of strict rules<br>严格规则的制定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charter contains a clear prescription of every member's duties.  该章程对每位成员的职责有明确规定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convention</td><td>/kənˈvenʃn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>常规，习俗；大会，集会</td><td>an annual convention<br>年度大会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands of teachers attended the annual convention held in the capital.  数千名教师参加了在首都举行的年度大会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 custom</td><td>/ˈkʌstəm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>习俗，惯例</td><td>a local custom<br>当地习俗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Removing your shoes indoors is an old custom in that country.  在那个国家，进屋脱鞋是古老的习俗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decree</td><td>/dɪˈkriː/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>法令，命令</td><td>issue a decree<br>颁布一项法令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government issued a decree banning imports of that timber.  政府颁布法令禁止进口那种木材。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 law</td><td>/ˈlɔː/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>法律，法规</td><td>pass a new law<br>通过一项新法律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parliament passed a new law protecting coastal wetlands.  议会通过了一项保护沿海湿地的新法律。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 regulation</td><td>/ˌreɡjəˈleɪʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>规章，规定</td><td>safety regulations<br>安全规章</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New safety regulations forced the factory to replace its machines.  新的安全规章迫使工厂更换机器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rule</td><td>/ˈruːl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>规则，规定</td><td>break the rules<br>违反规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Anyone who breaks the rules twice loses their membership.  任何人两次违反规定就会失去会员资格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>primitive</b></td><td>/ˈprɪmətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>原始的；简陋的；早期的</td><td>primitive tools<br>原始的工具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Archaeologists uncovered primitive stone tools beside the ancient riverbed.  考古学家在古河床旁发掘出原始的石制工具。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rudimentary</td><td>/ˌruːdɪˈmentri/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>初始的，未发展的；最根本的，基础的</td><td>rudimentary knowledge<br>基础的知识</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He had only a rudimentary knowledge of chemistry when he joined the lab.  他加入实验室时对化学只有基础的了解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elemental</td><td>/ˌeləˈmentəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>基本的，根本的</td><td>elemental human needs<br>人的基本需求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The story explores elemental fears of darkness and loss.  这个故事探讨对黑暗与失去的原始恐惧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 advanced</td><td>/ədˈvænst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>先进的，高级的</td><td>advanced technology<br>先进的技术</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lab uses advanced technology unavailable elsewhere in the country.  该实验室使用国内其他地方没有的先进技术。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 developed</td><td>/dɪˈveləpt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>发达的，成熟的</td><td>a highly developed economy<br>高度发达的经济</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The island became a highly developed economy within one generation.  这个岛在一代人之内成为高度发达的经济体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 evolved</td><td>/ɪˈvɑːlvd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>进化的，发展成熟的</td><td>a more evolved form<br>更为进化的形态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their later pottery represents a far more evolved technique.  他们后期的陶器代表着远为成熟的技艺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prestige</b></td><td>/preˈstiːʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>声望，威望；有威望的</td><td>enjoy great prestige<br>享有很高声望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old university still enjoys enormous prestige among employers worldwide.  这所老牌大学在全球雇主中仍享有极高声望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 credit</td><td>/ˈkredɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>表扬，赞扬；信任</td><td>take credit for<br>把功劳归于自己</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She deserves credit for keeping the project alive.  她值得称赞，因为她让这个项目坚持下来了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fame</td><td>/ˈfeɪm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>名声，声誉</td><td>rise to sudden fame<br>骤然成名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her fame spread quickly after the documentary aired nationwide.  纪录片在全国播出后，她的名声迅速传开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 influence</td><td>/ˈɪnfluəns/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>影响力，影响</td><td>exert great influence<br>施加巨大影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her teacher had a lasting influence on her career choices.  她的老师对她的职业选择有长久影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reputation</td><td>/ˌrepjəˈteɪʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>名声，声望</td><td>build a reputation<br>建立起声望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She built a reputation for honesty over twenty long years.  她用漫长的二十年建立起诚实的声望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infamy</td><td>/ˈɪnfəmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恶名，臭名；恶行</td><td>a day of infamy<br>耻辱的一天</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His name lives on in infamy after the scandal.  丑闻之后他的名字以恶名流传下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>presumptuous</b></td><td>/prɪˈzʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冒失的，放肆的</td><td>presumptuous of you<br>你太放肆了</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It seems presumptuous to criticize a book you have not read.  批评一本没读过的书，似乎太放肆了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 assumptive</del></td><td>/əˈsʌmptɪv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，自以为是的；假定的</td><td>an assumptive attitude<br>傲慢的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His assumptive manner annoyed everyone at the negotiating table.  他傲慢的态度让谈判桌上的人都很反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bumptious</td><td>/ˈbʌmpʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横傲慢的，自以为是的</td><td>a bumptious young man<br>自以为是的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her bumptious manner annoyed the older members of the committee.  她自以为是的态度让委员会里年长的成员很反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cavalier</td><td>/ˌkævəˈlɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>有骑士风度的人；傲慢轻率的</td><td>a cavalier attitude<br>傲慢轻率的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company took a cavalier attitude toward worker safety.  这家公司对工人安全的态度傲慢轻率。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 haughty</td><td>/ˈhɔːti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，目中无人的</td><td>a haughty manner<br>傲慢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the waiter a haughty look and said nothing.  她傲慢地看了服务员一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imperious</td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>an imperious tone<br>专横的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her imperious manner made junior colleagues afraid to disagree.  她专横的态度让年轻同事不敢有异议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lofty</td><td>/ˈlɔːfti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>崇高的；自大的</td><td>a lofty tone<br>自大的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dismissed our idea with a lofty wave of her hand.  她自大地一挥手就否定了我们的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 overweening</td><td>/ˌoʊvərˈwiːnɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傲慢的，专横的</td><td>overweening pride<br>过度的骄傲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His overweening confidence led him to ignore every warning from the engineers.  他过度的自信使他忽视了工程师的每一次警告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peremptory</td><td>/pəˈremptəri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不容置疑的；专横傲慢的</td><td>a peremptory order<br>不容置疑的命令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His peremptory tone left no room for questions or discussion.  他傲慢的语气不给任何提问或讨论的余地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 supercilious</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈsɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>高傲的，傲慢的</td><td>a supercilious smile<br>傲慢的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A supercilious smile crossed his face when he heard my question.  听到我的问题，他脸上掠过傲慢的微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modest</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谦虚的；不大的，适度的</td><td>modest about her success<br>对成功很谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed modest about his award and thanked his team.  他对自己的奖项很谦虚，还感谢了团队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pretense</b></td><td>/ˈpriːtens/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>假装，伪装；借口</td><td>under false pretenses<br>以虚假的名义</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He obtained the money under false pretenses and was arrested months later.  他以虚假的名义骗取钱财，几个月后被逮捕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affectation</td><td>/ˌæfekˈteɪʃn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>虚伪，做作</td><td>an obvious affectation<br>明显的矫饰做作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His British accent struck everyone as a rather transparent affectation.  在众人看来，他的英式口音是相当明显的做作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 camouflage</td><td>/ˈkæməflɑːʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>伪装，掩饰；伪装物</td><td>military camouflage<br>军事伪装</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soldiers used branches and mud as camouflage in the forest.  士兵们用树枝和泥浆在森林里作伪装。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deceit</td><td>/dəˈsiːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>欺骗，欺诈</td><td>a web of deceit<br>一张欺骗的网</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His whole story turned out to be a web of deceit.  他整个说法后来证明是一张欺骗之网。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disguise</td><td>/dɪsˈɡaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>伪装，掩饰；伪装</td><td>disguise his intentions<br>掩饰他的意图</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He disguised his disappointment behind a polite and steady smile.  他用礼貌平静的微笑掩饰了失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imposture</td><td>/ɪmˈpɑstʃər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冒名顶替，欺骗</td><td>expose an imposture<br>揭露一场冒名骗局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single letter exposed the imposture that had fooled everyone.  一封信揭露了骗过所有人的冒名行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mask</td><td>/ˈmæsk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>掩盖，掩饰；面具</td><td>mask his anger<br>掩饰他的怒气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud music masked the sound of the approaching helicopter.  响亮的音乐掩盖了直升机靠近的声音。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 masquerade</td><td>/ˌmæskəˈreɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>面具，伪装</td><td>a masquerade of concern<br>假装关心的伪装</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His politeness was only a masquerade concealing deep resentment toward his colleagues.  他的客气只是掩盖对同事深深怨恨的伪装。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assumption</td><td>/əˈsʌmpʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自大，傲慢；假设</td><td>an air of assumption<br>自大的神气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His quiet assumption of authority annoyed the older members.  他不声不响地自居权威，惹恼了年长成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hauteur</td><td>/hɔːˈtɜːr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>傲慢，自大</td><td>an air of hauteur<br>傲慢的神态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her hauteur offended the very people whose support she needed most.  她的傲慢恰恰得罪了她最需要支持的那些人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 imperiousness</del></td><td>/ɪmˈpɪriəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专横，霸道</td><td>the imperiousness of power<br>权力带来的专横</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His imperiousness in meetings silenced every junior colleague.  他在会上的专横让每个年轻同事都不敢开口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 loftiness</del></td><td>/ˈlɔftinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>高傲，自大</td><td>intellectual loftiness<br>智识上的高傲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke with a loftiness that irritated the waiting reporters.  她说话时的高傲让等候的记者们不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 lordliness</del></td><td>/ˈlɔrdlinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>傲慢，自命高贵</td><td>insufferable lordliness<br>令人难忍的傲慢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lordliness toward the staff cost him their loyalty.  他对员工的傲慢让他失去了他们的忠诚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pomposity</td><td>/pɑːmˈpɑːsəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>自大，浮夸</td><td>academic pomposity<br>学术上的浮夸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech was ruined by pomposity and empty phrases.  演讲被浮夸和空话毁了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 superciliousness</del></td><td>/ˌsupərˈsɪliəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>傲慢，目中无人</td><td>chilly superciliousness<br>冷冰冰的傲慢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic's superciliousness annoyed even his most loyal readers.  这位评论家的傲慢连最忠实的读者都反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 superiority</td><td>/səˌpɪriˈɔːrəti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>优越，优越感</td><td>an air of superiority<br>优越感的姿态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His tone of superiority made the new staff uncomfortable.  他那种优越的语气让新员工很不舒服。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sincerity</td><td>/sɪnˈserəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>真诚，诚挚</td><td>speak with sincerity<br>真诚地说话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one doubted the sincerity of her apology that evening.  那天晚上没人怀疑她道歉的真诚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humility</td><td>/hjuˈmɪlɪti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦逊，谦卑</td><td>accept with humility<br>谦逊地接受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He accepted the prize with genuine humility and short thanks.  他以真诚的谦逊和简短的致谢接受了奖项。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 modesty</td><td>/ˈmɑːdəsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>谦虚，谦逊</td><td>false modesty<br>假谦虚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her modesty about the discovery impressed the whole committee.  她对这一发现的谦逊让整个委员会印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>preternatural</b></td><td>/ˌpriːtərˈnætʃrəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>超乎寻常的</td><td>preternatural calm<br>异乎寻常的平静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pilot showed preternatural calm while the engine was clearly failing.  发动机明显故障时，飞行员表现出超乎寻常的镇定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aberrant</td><td>/æˈberənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>异常的，非常规的</td><td>aberrant behavior<br>异常行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors were puzzled by the patient's aberrant test results.  医生对病人异常的化验结果感到困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abnormal</td><td>/æbˈnɔːrməl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>异常的，不正常的</td><td>abnormal test results<br>异常的检测结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scan showed nothing abnormal in her lungs.  扫描显示她的肺部没有异常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 anomalous</td><td>/əˈnɑːmələs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不平常的，异常的</td><td>anomalous readings<br>异常的读数</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team discarded a few anomalous readings from the data.  团队剔除了数据中几个异常读数。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extraordinary</td><td>/ɪkˈstrɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非凡的，特别的</td><td>extraordinary courage<br>非凡的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took extraordinary courage to report what she had seen.  举报她所见之事需要非凡的勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 phenomenal</td><td>/fəˈnɑːmɪnl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不寻常的，非凡的</td><td>phenomenal success<br>非凡的成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel enjoyed phenomenal success in a dozen countries.  她的第一部小说在十几个国家取得了非凡的成功。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magical</td><td>/ˈmædʒɪkəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>魔法的，神奇的</td><td>a magical atmosphere<br>神奇的氛围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Candlelight gave the small chapel a magical atmosphere.  烛光让这座小教堂有了神奇的氛围。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 miraculous</td><td>/mɪˈrækjələs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇迹般的，超乎自然的</td><td>a miraculous recovery<br>奇迹般的康复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors called her recovery nothing short of miraculous.  医生称她的康复简直是奇迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unearthly</td><td>/ʌnˈɜːrθli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非尘世的，诡异恐怖的</td><td>an unearthly silence<br>诡异的寂静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An unearthly cry came from somewhere deep in the forest.  森林深处传来一声诡异的叫声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 common</td><td>/ˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，常见的；共有的</td><td>a common mistake<br>常见的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Spelling errors are a common problem in student essays.  拼写错误是学生作文中常见的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ordinary</td><td>/ˈɔːrdəneri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普通的，平常的</td><td>just an ordinary weekday<br>只是平常的工作日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked like an ordinary commuter waiting for the morning bus.  他看上去就像个等早班公交的普通上班族。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prosaic</td><td>/prəˈzeɪɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>单调的，常见的</td><td>prosaic details<br>平淡的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Behind the legend lies a prosaic story about a lost receipt.  传说背后是一个关于遗失收据的平淡故事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prognosis</b></td><td>/prɑːɡˈnoʊsɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>预后；预测；前景判断</td><td>a gloomy prognosis<br>悲观的预测</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctors offered a gloomy prognosis after reviewing the scans.  医生看过扫描片后给出了悲观的预后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 augury</td><td>/ˈɔɡjəri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>预兆；占卜</td><td>a good augury for the future<br>未来的好预兆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clear sunrise was taken as a favorable augury.  晴朗的日出被视为吉兆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 forecast</td><td>/ˈfɔːrˌkæst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>预测，预报</td><td>forecast heavy rain<br>预报大雨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Economists forecast slower growth for the coming two years.  经济学家预测未来两年增长会放缓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foretelling</td><td>/fɔrˈtelɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>预言，预示</td><td>the foretelling of disaster<br>对灾难的预言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her foretelling of the drought proved uncomfortably accurate.  她对旱灾的预言准得让人不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 predicting</td><td>/prɪˈdɪktɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>预测，预言</td><td>predicting the weather<br>预测天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Predicting the weather beyond ten days remains largely guesswork.  预测十天以上的天气基本上仍是猜测。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presaging</td><td>/ˈpresɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>预示，预兆</td><td>the presaging of trouble<br>麻烦的预兆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dark clouds gathered, presaging a long and violent storm.  乌云聚集，预示一场漫长猛烈的风暴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prediction</td><td>/priˈdɪkʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>预测，预言</td><td>an accurate prediction<br>准确的预测</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His prediction about the election proved surprisingly accurate.  他对选举的预测准得出人意料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prophecy</td><td>/ˈprɑːfəsi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>预言，预示</td><td>fulfill a prophecy<br>使预言成真</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old prophecy seemed to fulfill itself within a decade.  那个古老的预言似乎在十年内自我实现了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prevalent</b></td><td>/ˈprevələnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>流行的，普遍的</td><td>a prevalent belief<br>普遍的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The belief that talent is innate remains surprisingly prevalent among teachers.  认为天赋是天生的这种看法在教师中仍出奇地普遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conventional</td><td>/kənˈvenʃənəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>传统的，常规的</td><td>conventional wisdom<br>传统观念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They chose a conventional design to please the older members.  他们选了常规设计以取悦年长的成员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dominant</td><td>/ˈdɑːmɪnənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>处于支配地位的；（基因）显性的</td><td>the dominant player<br>占主导地位的一方</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dominant player in the market set prices that everyone else followed.  市场上占主导地位的那家定的价，其他人都跟着走。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 common</td><td>/ˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，常见的；共有的</td><td>a common mistake<br>常见的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Spelling errors are a common problem in student essays.  拼写错误是学生作文中常见的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 popular</td><td>/ˈpɑːpjələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>流行的，普遍的</td><td>a popular belief<br>普遍流行的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a popular belief that the well never dried.  当时普遍认为那口井从不干涸。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 predominant</td><td>/prɪˈdɑːmənənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>主要的，占优势的</td><td>the predominant view<br>占主导的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rice remains the predominant crop in the southern valleys.  稻米仍是南部谷地的主要作物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preponderant</td><td>/prɪˈpɑndərənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>占优势的，主要的</td><td>the preponderant influence<br>占主导的影响力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trade remained the preponderant concern in every round of talks.  贸易在每一轮会谈中都是最主要的议题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prevailing</td><td>/prɪˈveɪlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，占主导的</td><td>the prevailing view<br>占主导的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prevailing view among historians has changed considerably since then.  历史学家中占主导的看法自那以后大为改变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rife</td><td>/raɪf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的；充斥的；流行的</td><td>rife with rumors<br>充斥着传言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small town was rife with rumors about the mayor's resignation.  小镇上充斥着关于市长辞职的谣言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 absent</td><td>/ˈæbsənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺席的，不存在的</td><td>largely absent from<br>在…中基本不存在</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Such customs are entirely absent from the northern villages.  这类习俗在北方村庄完全不存在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rare</td><td>/ˈrer/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀少的，罕见的</td><td>a rare bird<br>罕见的鸟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is rare to see snow here in April.  四月这里下雪很罕见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prevaricate</b></td><td>/prɪˈværɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>支吾其词，撒谎</td><td>prevaricate about the facts<br>就事实支吾其词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Witnesses began to prevaricate whenever the lawyer asked about the missing money.  每当律师问到失踪的钱，证人就开始支吾其词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 equivocate</td><td>/ɪˈkwɪvəkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（带有欺骗目的地）模棱两可地说，说谎话</td><td>equivocate under questioning<br>受问时含糊其辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She equivocated under questioning about the true state of her finances.  在被问及财务真实状况时，她含糊其辞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fabricate</td><td>/ˈfæbrɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>捏造</td><td>fabricate evidence<br>伪造证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness had fabricated evidence to protect his own brother.  那名证人捏造证据来保护自己的兄弟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 falsify</td><td>/ˈfɔːlsəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>篡改，伪造；证明为假</td><td>falsify the records<br>篡改记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An accountant was accused of falsifying the company's records.  一名会计被指控篡改公司账目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lie</td><td>/ˈlaɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>谎言；说谎</td><td>tell a lie<br>说谎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told an obvious lie about where he had been.  他就自己去过哪里说了个明显的谎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 palter</del></td><td>/ˈpɔːltər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欺骗；讨价还价</td><td>palter with the truth<br>对真相含糊其词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Witnesses should not palter with the truth when giving sworn testimony.  证人在宣誓作证时不应对真相含糊其词。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>primordial</b></td><td>/praɪˈmɔːrdiəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>原始的，最初的</td><td>primordial soup<br>原始生命之汤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Life may have begun in a primordial soup of simple chemicals.  生命可能始于由简单化学物质构成的原始之汤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ancient</td><td>/ˈeɪntʃənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>古代的，古老的</td><td>ancient civilizations<br>古代文明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ancient civilizations along this river left almost no writing.  这条河沿岸的古代文明几乎没留下文字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 early</td><td>/ˈɜːrli/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>早的，提前的</td><td>an early talent<br>早年显露的才华</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her early talent for languages surprised all of her teachers.  她早年显露的语言才华让所有老师惊讶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 primal</td><td>/ˈpraɪməl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>原始的；最初的，首要的</td><td>a primal instinct<br>一种原始本能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fear of the dark is a primal human response.  怕黑是人类一种原始的反应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 primeval</td><td>/praɪˈmiːvəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>原始的，太古的</td><td>primeval forest<br>原始森林</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A patch of primeval forest still survives on the slope.  山坡上仍存留着一小片原始森林。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 primitive</td><td>/ˈprɪmətɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>原始的；简陋的；早期的</td><td>primitive tools<br>原始的工具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Archaeologists uncovered primitive stone tools beside the ancient riverbed.  考古学家在古河床旁发掘出原始的石制工具。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 late</td><td>/ˈleɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>晚的，迟的；已故的</td><td>late in the season<br>在季节的后期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rain came too late to save the withered crops.  雨来得太晚，救不了枯萎的庄稼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 recent</td><td>/ˈriːsənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>最近的，近来的</td><td>in recent years<br>近几年来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Recent studies suggest that short naps improve memory.  最近的研究表明小睡能改善记忆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>primp</b></td><td>/prɪmp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>精心打扮</td><td>primp for the party<br>为聚会精心打扮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spent an hour primping in front of the mirror before the party.  聚会前她在镜子前精心打扮了一个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preen</td><td>/priːn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>打扮修饰</td><td>preen before the mirror<br>在镜前打扮修饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer paused to preen before the tall mirror backstage.  那位舞者在后台的高镜前停下来打扮修饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dress up</td><td>/drɛs ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>打扮，装扮；美化</td><td>dress up as a pirate<br>装扮成海盗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children dressed up as ghosts for the school party.  孩子们打扮成鬼怪去参加学校晚会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>peddle</b></td><td>/ˈpedl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>叫卖，兜售</td><td>peddle goods door to door<br>挨家挨户兜售货物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He used to peddle brushes and soap from door to door.  他过去挨家挨户兜售刷子和肥皂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hawk</td><td>/ˈhɔːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>叫卖，沿街推销</td><td>hawk cheap souvenirs<br>叫卖廉价纪念品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vendors hawked cheap souvenirs outside the crowded temple gate.  小贩们在拥挤的庙门外叫卖廉价纪念品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>pristine</b></td><td>/prɪˈstiːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>崭新的；纯净的；原始的</td><td>pristine white snow<br>洁白无垢的雪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cabin sat beside a pristine lake untouched by development.  那间小屋坐落在一个未受开发影响的纯净湖泊旁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immaculate</td><td>/ɪˈmækjələt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>洁净无瑕的；完美无缺的</td><td>an immaculate record<br>毫无瑕疵的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After thirty years of service his record remained absolutely immaculate.  服务三十年后，他的记录依然毫无瑕疵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spotless</td><td>/ˈspɑːtləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>一点污迹也没有的</td><td>a spotless kitchen<br>一尘不染的厨房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her spotless kitchen shone under the bright evening light.  她一尘不染的厨房在明亮的夜灯下发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stainless</td><td>/ˈsteɪnləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无污渍的；不锈的</td><td>stainless steel pots<br>不锈钢锅具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The stainless surface showed not one mark after cooking.  烹饪后这块不锈表面没有一点痕迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unsoiled</del></td><td>/ˌʌnˈsɔɪld/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未被弄脏的，洁净的</td><td>unsoiled bandages<br>洁净未污的绷带</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only one unsoiled napkin remained by the end of dinner.  晚餐结束时只剩一张未弄脏的餐巾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unstained</td><td>/ʌnˈsteɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无污渍的；清白的</td><td>an unstained record<br>毫无污点的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He retired after forty unstained years of public service.  他在四十年毫无污点的公职之后退休。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsullied</td><td>/ʌnˈsʌlid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>未被玷污的，纯洁的</td><td>an unsullied reputation<br>未被玷污的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unsullied honesty made her the obvious choice for treasurer.  她未受玷污的诚实使她成为财务的当然人选。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tainted</td><td>/ˈteɪntɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>被污染的，有污点的</td><td>tainted water supply<br>被污染的水源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A tainted water supply sickened dozens of people last month.  上月被污染的水源使数十人生病。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 squalid</td><td>/ˈskwɑːlɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>污秽的，肮脏的</td><td>squalid living conditions<br>肮脏的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The refugees endured squalid living conditions in the overcrowded camp.  难民在过度拥挤的营地里忍受肮脏的居住条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 contaminated</td><td>/kənˈtæməˌneɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>受污染的</td><td>contaminated soil<br>受污染的土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers removed several tons of contaminated soil from the site.  工人们从场地运走了数吨受污染的土壤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 besmirched</td><td>/bɪˈsmɜrtʃt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>被弄脏的；名声受损的</td><td>a besmirched reputation<br>受损的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless lie left his besmirched name beyond repair.  一句大意的谎言让他受损的名声无法挽回。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 corrupted by civilization</td><td>/kəˈrʌptɪd baɪ ˌsɪvələˈzeɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>被文明所败坏的</td><td>wholly corrupted by civilization<br>完全被文明败坏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He imagined a valley not yet corrupted by civilization.  他想象着一处尚未被文明败坏的山谷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>privation</b></td><td>/praɪˈveɪʃn/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>缺乏，穷困</td><td>years of privation<br>多年的穷困匮乏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of privation had left the villagers thin and utterly exhausted.  多年的穷困匮乏使村民们瘦弱不堪、极度疲惫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deprivation</td><td>/ˌdeprɪˈveɪʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>剥夺；匮乏</td><td>sleep deprivation<br>睡眠剥夺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of sleep deprivation left the young doctor exhausted and irritable.  多年的睡眠剥夺让这位年轻医生疲惫易怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loss</td><td>/ˈlɔːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>丧失，损失</td><td>the loss of income<br>收入的丧失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The loss of income forced them to sell the farm.  收入的丧失迫使他们卖掉农场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 repletion</del></td><td>/rɪˈpliʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>充裕，饱足</td><td>eat to repletion<br>吃到饱足为止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guests ate to repletion and then dozed by the fire.  客人们吃到饱足，然后在炉火边打起了瞌睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>probe</b></td><td>/proʊb/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>深入调查</td><td>probe deeply into the scandal<br>深入调查这桩丑闻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investigators continued to probe deeply into the company's missing funds.  调查人员继续深入调查公司资金失踪的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delve</td><td>/delv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>探究，钻研</td><td>delve into ancient records<br>钻研古代记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She delved into the family archives and found a startling old letter.  她钻研家族档案，发现了一封令人震惊的旧信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 explore</td><td>/ɪksˈplɔːr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>探索，深入研究</td><td>explore every possibility<br>探究每一种可能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee explored every possibility before making its decision.  委员会在做决定前探究了每一种可能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inquire</td><td>/ɪnˈkwaɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>询问，查问</td><td>inquire about the price<br>询问价格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She inquired politely about the train's departure time.  她礼貌地询问列车发车时间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delving</td><td>/ˈdelvɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>深入探究，钻研</td><td>delving into old records<br>钻研旧档案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her patient delving into archives uncovered the forgotten letters.  她耐心钻研档案，发现了被遗忘的信件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disquisition</td><td>/ˌdɪskwɪˈzɪʃn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>专题论文</td><td>a long disquisition on art<br>关于艺术的长篇论述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He delivered a long disquisition on art that lasted well past midnight.  他就艺术作了长篇论述，一直讲到午夜之后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exploration</td><td>/ˌekspləˈreɪʃən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>探索，考察</td><td>the exploration of a theme<br>对某主题的探索</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The book is a careful exploration of rural childhood memory.  这本书是对乡村童年记忆的细致探索。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inquisition</td><td>/ˌɪnkwəˈzɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>严厉的审问；调查</td><td>a harsh inquisition<br>严厉的审问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The interview felt less like a chat than an inquisition.  这次面谈不像聊天，倒像审问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>probity</b></td><td>/ˈproʊbəti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直</td><td>unquestioned probity<br>毋庸置疑的正直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge was known for probity and unfailing patience.  这位法官以正直和永不失去的耐心闻名。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 honesty</td><td>/ˈɑːnəsti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>诚实，正直</td><td>honesty in business<br>生意上的诚信</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her honesty about the mistake earned everyone's respect.  她对错误的坦诚赢得了所有人的尊重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 integrity</td><td>/ɪnˈteɡrəti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直；完整性</td><td>a man of integrity<br>正直的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone respected the judge as a man of unshakable integrity and courage.  所有人都尊敬这位法官，视他为正直不屈且勇气可嘉的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rectitude</td><td>/ˈrektɪtuːd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直；品行端正</td><td>moral rectitude<br>道德上的正直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old judge was admired for his moral rectitude and quiet courage.  这位老法官因道德上的正直和沉静的勇气而受人敬仰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 righteousness</td><td>/ˈraɪtʃəsnəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直，正义</td><td>a sense of righteousness<br>正义感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He defended the poor with a quiet righteousness that impressed everyone.  他以一种沉静的正义感为穷人辩护，令所有人钦佩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uprightness</td><td>/ˈʌpraɪtnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>正直，诚实</td><td>moral uprightness<br>道德上的正直</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His moral uprightness earned respect even from his opponents.  他道德上的正直连对手都尊敬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unscrupulousness</del></td><td>/ʌnˈskrupjələsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不择手段，无原则</td><td>shocking unscrupulousness<br>令人震惊的无原则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dealer's unscrupulousness eventually destroyed his own business.  这个商人的不择手段最终毁了他自己的生意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 shiftiness</del></td><td>/ˈʃɪftinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狡诈，不可靠</td><td>an air of shiftiness<br>一副狡诈相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His shiftiness during the interview made the panel distrust every answer.  他面试时的狡诈做派让评委不信任他的每个回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 baseness</td><td>/ˈbeɪsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>卑劣，下贱</td><td>the baseness of betrayal<br>背叛的卑劣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play exposes the baseness hidden beneath polite manners.  这部剧揭露礼貌举止下隐藏的卑劣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dishonor</td><td>/dɪsˈɑːnər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使丢脸，玷污；不光彩</td><td>dishonor his memory<br>玷污他的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lying under oath would dishonor everything he had defended.  作伪证会玷污他一生所维护的一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 lowness</del></td><td>/ˈloʊnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>卑劣，下贱</td><td>moral lowness<br>道德卑劣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lowness of his tactics shocked even his longtime political allies.  他手段之卑劣连长期的政治盟友都感到震惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>proclivity</b></td><td>/prəˈklɪvəti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>癖性，偏好（多指不良倾向）</td><td>criminal proclivities<br>犯罪倾向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His proclivity for gambling eventually ruined the family.  他的赌博癖好最终毁了这个家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aptitude</td><td>/ˈæptəˌtuːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>天资，天生的倾向</td><td>an aptitude for languages<br>语言天赋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She showed an early aptitude for languages and mastered three by twelve.  她很早显出语言天赋，十二岁前就掌握了三门语言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disposition</td><td>/ˌdɪspəˈzɪʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>倾向，性情</td><td>a disposition to argue<br>爱争辩的倾向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a natural disposition to question every rule.  她天生有质疑每条规则的倾向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 leaning</td><td>/ˈliːnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>倾斜的；倾向</td><td>a leaning tower<br>倾斜的塔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The leaning wall was propped up with heavy timbers.  倾斜的墙用粗木支撑起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 partiality</td><td>/ˌpɑːrʃiˈæləti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>偏爱；偏袒</td><td>a partiality for sweets<br>对甜食的偏爱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach was accused of partiality toward his own students.  教练被指责偏袒自己的学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 penchant</td><td>/ˈpentʃənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>嗜好，强烈爱好</td><td>a penchant for luxury<br>对奢华的偏好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her penchant for dramatic hats made her instantly recognizable.  她对夸张帽子的偏爱让人一眼就认出她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 predilection</td><td>/ˌpredəˈlekʃən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>偏爱，偏好</td><td>a predilection for jazz<br>对爵士乐的偏爱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic's predilection for older films colored every review.  这位评论家对老电影的偏爱影响了他的每篇评论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 propensity</td><td>/prəˈpensəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>倾向，癖好</td><td>a propensity to exaggerate<br>夸大的倾向</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy has a propensity to lose every umbrella he owns.  这男孩总有把伞丢掉的习性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aversion</td><td>/əˈvɜːrʒən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，反感</td><td>a strong aversion to<br>对…强烈厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has a lifelong aversion to crowded parties.  他一生都厌恶拥挤的聚会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 antipathy</td><td>/ænˈtɪpəθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>厌恶，反感</td><td>mutual antipathy<br>相互反感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There has long been an antipathy between the two departments.  这两个部门之间长期存在反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disinclination</td><td>/ˌdɪsˌɪnklɪˈneɪʃn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不愿，无意</td><td>a disinclination to travel<br>不愿出行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His disinclination to speak in public cost him the promotion.  他不愿公开发言使他失去了升职机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>procrastinate</b></td><td>/proʊˈkræstɪneɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>拖延，耽误</td><td>tend to procrastinate<br>习惯拖延</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I always procrastinate when the deadline still feels comfortably far away.  截止日期还远的时候我总会拖延。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>procure</b></td><td>/prəˈkjʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>获得，取得</td><td>procure weapons illegally<br>非法获取武器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The smugglers tried to procure weapons through a network of middlemen.  走私者试图通过中间人网络获取武器。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 acquire</td><td>/əˈkwaɪər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>获得；取得；逐渐养成</td><td>acquire a taste for<br>逐渐喜欢上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took years for him to acquire a taste for olives.  他花了好几年才慢慢喜欢上橄榄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attain</td><td>/əˈteɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>达到，获得</td><td>attain a goal<br>达成目标</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She attained her doctorate before turning twenty-six.  她在二十六岁前取得了博士学位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 garner</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrnər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>获得，积累</td><td>garner support<br>获得支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal garnered support from both major parties.  该提案获得了两大党的支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knock down</td><td>/ˈnɑk ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>拆卸；击倒</td><td>knock down the shed<br>拆掉棚屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They knocked down the old barn and sold the timber.  他们拆掉了旧谷仓并卖掉了木料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pull down</td><td>/pʊl ˈdaʊn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>挣得（钱）；拆毁</td><td>pull down a salary<br>挣得薪水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He pulls down a decent salary writing code for a small startup.  他在一家小创业公司写代码，挣得一份不错的薪水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bring in</td><td>/brɪŋ ˈɪn/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>带来（收入）；引进</td><td>bring in extra income<br>带来额外收入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The weekend market brings in more cash than the whole weekday trade.  周末集市带来的现金比整个工作日的生意还多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 relinquish</td><td>/rɪˈlɪŋkwɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>放弃（权力、职位）</td><td>relinquish control<br>放弃控制权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She refused to relinquish control of the family business.  她拒绝放弃对家族企业的控制权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 forfeit</td><td>/ˈfɔːrfət/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>丧失，被没收（权利、财物）</td><td>forfeit the right to appeal<br>丧失上诉权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He forfeited his right to appeal by missing the deadline.  他因错过期限而丧失了上诉权。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lose</td><td>/ˈluːz/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>失去；丢失</td><td>lose everything overnight<br>一夜间失去一切</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careless investors can lose everything overnight when a bubble finally bursts.  泡沫破裂时，粗心的投资者可能一夜之间失去一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prod</b></td><td>/prɑːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>促使…行动</td><td>prod her into action<br>促使她行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her friends had to prod her into applying for the scholarship.  朋友们不得不促使她去申请那份奖学金。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 encourage</td><td>/ɪnˈkɜːrɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>鼓励，激励；促进</td><td>encourage students to read<br>鼓励学生阅读</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Good teachers encourage students to ask difficult questions.  好老师鼓励学生提出有难度的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exhort</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːrt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>敦促，力劝</td><td>exhort them to persevere<br>力劝他们坚持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speaker exhorted the students to persevere despite repeated failures.  演讲者勉励学生们即使屡遭失败也要坚持下去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 goad</td><td>/ɡoʊd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>刺激，驱使，激发</td><td>goad him into action<br>激他采取行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His teammates goaded him into accepting the ridiculous bet.  队友们激他接受了那个荒唐的赌约。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nudge</td><td>/nʌdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>说服某人做某事</td><td>nudge him into agreeing<br>说服他同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her patient questions nudged him into agreeing to see a doctor.  她耐心的询问说服他同意去看医生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prompt</td><td>/ˈprɑːmpt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>促使，引起；提示</td><td>prompt an inquiry<br>引发一场调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The leaked memo prompted an immediate inquiry by regulators.  泄露的备忘录促使监管机构立即展开调查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spur</td><td>/spɜːr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>刺激物</td><td>a spur to growth<br>增长的刺激因素</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rising exports acted as a powerful spur to industrial growth.  出口增长成为工业发展的强大刺激因素。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 egg on</td><td>/ɛɡ ˈɑn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>煽动，鼓动</td><td>egg him on<br>鼓动他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His friends egged him on until he agreed to the dare.  朋友们不断鼓动，直到他答应那个挑战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rein</td><td>/reɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>抑制，限制</td><td>keep a tight rein<br>严加约束</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new director kept a tight rein on every department's spending.  新任主管对各部门的开支严加约束。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prodigal</b></td><td>/ˈprɑːdɪɡl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>挥霍的</td><td>prodigal spending<br>挥霍无度的开销</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His prodigal spending emptied the family fortune within a few years.  他挥霍无度的花钱方式几年内就耗尽了家产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extravagant</td><td>/ɪkˈstrævəɡənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挥霍的；过度的，不必要的</td><td>extravagant spending<br>挥霍的开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their extravagant spending on parties left the charity nearly broke.  他们在宴会上的挥霍开支让这家慈善机构几乎破产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profligate</td><td>/ˈprɑːflɪɡət/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>挥金如土的，挥霍的</td><td>profligate spending<br>挥霍无度的开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council's profligate spending on ceremonies angered local taxpayers.  议会在典礼上挥霍无度的开支激怒了当地纳税人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squandering</td><td>/ˈskwɑːndərɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>浪费的，挥霍的</td><td>squandering public funds<br>挥霍公款</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Squandering money on gadgets left him broke by December.  在小玩意上挥霍钱财让他十二月就身无分文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unthrifty</del></td><td>/ʌnˈθrɪfti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不节俭的，挥霍的</td><td>an unthrifty heir<br>挥霍的继承人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unthrifty young lord sold off the estate piece by piece.  这位挥霍的年轻贵族把地产一块块卖掉了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wasteful</td><td>/ˈweɪstfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浪费的，挥霍的</td><td>wasteful of resources<br>浪费资源的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Printing every email is wasteful and completely unnecessary.  把每封邮件都打印出来既浪费又完全没必要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 fritterer</del></td><td>/ˈfrɪtərər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>浪费时间或金钱的人</td><td>a hopeless fritterer<br>无可救药的挥霍者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her brother is a fritterer who wastes both his savings and his talent.  她弟弟是个挥霍者，既浪费积蓄也浪费才华。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 highroller</del></td><td>/ˈhaɪ ˌroʊlər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>挥金如土的人；豪赌客</td><td>a Las Vegas highroller<br>拉斯维加斯豪客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The casino treats every highroller to a free suite and private car.  赌场为每位豪客提供免费套房和专车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spender</td><td>/ˈspendər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>花钱的人；挥霍者</td><td>a big spender<br>花钱大手的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My uncle is a big spender who never checks his bank balance.  我叔叔花钱大手大脚，从不看银行余额。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spendthrift</td><td>/ˈspendθrɪft/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>挥霍者，败家子</td><td>a reckless spendthrift<br>肆意挥霍的败家子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A reckless spendthrift, he owed money to nearly every friend.  作为一个挥霍无度的人，他几乎欠了每个朋友的钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 squanderer</del></td><td>/ˈskwɑndərər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>浪费者，挥霍者</td><td>a squanderer of talent<br>浪费才华的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 History remembers him as a squanderer of a vast family fortune.  历史把他记作败掉万贯家财的挥霍者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waster</td><td>/ˈweɪstər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>挥霍者；无用之人</td><td>an idle waster<br>游手好闲的败家子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His father called him a waster who had thrown away every chance.  他父亲说他是个败家子，白白丢掉了每个机会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wastrel</td><td>/ˈweɪstrəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>肆意挥霍的人，败家子</td><td>a drunken wastrel<br>酗酒的败家子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family fortune was spent by a wastrel who gambled nightly.  家族财产被一个夜夜赌博的败家子花光了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 frugal</td><td>/ˈfruːɡl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节俭的；节省的；朴素的</td><td>a frugal lifestyle<br>节俭的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They led a frugal life and saved almost every coin.  他们过着节俭的生活，几乎每一分钱都存起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 parsimonious</td><td>/ˌpɑːrsəˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，过分节俭的</td><td>parsimonious with praise<br>极少给出赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The parsimonious landlord refused to replace the broken heater.  吝啬的房东拒绝更换坏了的暖气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 conserving</td><td>/kənˈsɜːrvɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节约的，保存的</td><td>conserving energy<br>节约能源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple habits like conserving water reduce the monthly bills.  节约用水这类简单习惯能降低每月账单。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 economical</td><td>/ˌekəˈnɑːmɪkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>节约的，经济的</td><td>an economical car<br>省油的汽车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A small diesel engine is more economical on long journeys.  小型柴油发动机长途出行更省油。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 economizing</td><td>/ɪˈkɑːnəˌmaɪzɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>节约的，省俭的</td><td>economizing on fuel<br>节省燃料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 By economizing on small daily luxuries, they paid off the loan early.  靠在日常小奢侈上省俭，他们提前还清了贷款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 penny-pinching</td><td>/ˈpɛni ˌpɪntʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吝啬的，抠门的</td><td>penny-pinching habits<br>抠门的习惯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His penny-pinching ways annoyed everyone at the restaurant.  他抠门的做法让餐馆里所有人都不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scrimping</td><td>/ˈskrɪmpɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>省吃俭用的，节省的</td><td>scrimping on food<br>在吃上省钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After years of scrimping, they finally bought a small house.  省吃俭用多年后，他们终于买了一栋小房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 skimping</td><td>/ˈskɪmpɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>克扣的，偷工减料的</td><td>skimping on materials<br>在材料上偷工减料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Skimping on safety checks eventually caused the accident.  在安全检查上偷工减料最终导致了事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 thrifty</td><td>/ˈθrɪfti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>节俭的</td><td>a thrifty shopper<br>节俭的购物者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thrifty habits helped her pay off the loan early.  节俭的习惯帮她提前还清了贷款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 penury</td><td>/ˈpenjəri/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>贫穷；吝啬，节俭</td><td>live in penury<br>生活贫困</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The once wealthy family ended its days in complete penury.  这个曾经富裕的家庭最终在彻底的贫困中度日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>prodigious</b></td><td>/prəˈdɪdʒəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；惊人的，了不起的</td><td>a prodigious memory<br>惊人的记忆力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pianist has a prodigious memory for long and complicated scores.  这位钢琴家对冗长复杂的乐谱有惊人的记忆力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 colossal</td><td>/kəˈlɑːsl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的</td><td>a colossal statue<br>巨大的雕像</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A colossal statue of the founder stands in the square.  广场上矗立着创始人的巨大雕像。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elephantine</td><td>/ˌelɪˈfæntiːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；笨拙的</td><td>elephantine movements<br>笨重的动作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His elephantine movements on stage made the delicate dance look almost comic.  他在台上笨重的动作让这支细腻的舞蹈显得近乎滑稽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enormous</td><td>/ɪˈnɔːrməs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，庞大的</td><td>an enormous amount of data<br>庞大的数据量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project required an enormous amount of money and patience.  这个项目需要巨额资金和极大耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gigantic</td><td>/dʒaɪˈɡæntɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的</td><td>a gigantic stone statue<br>巨大的石像</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A gigantic stone statue guards the entrance to the temple.  一座巨大的石像守卫着神庙的入口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
